So, yeah, focusing is definitely a big issue for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling disoriented or unsure of what's going on around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I always know where I am and what's happening around me. It's not like I feel lost... it's really just that my brain won't hold onto a thought. But I'm never actually confused about my surroundings or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused about things that are happening around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was just saying, I'm not disoriented or anything. I'm not confused about what's going on... it's just the focusing and the memory stuff that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's not like a noise or people walking down the hall pull my attention away. The problem is more... internal, I guess. My brain just kind of loses track of what I'm doing on its own, rather than me actually getting distracted by my surroundings.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble learning new things?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been trying to learn anything new lately. I just do my job cleaning the building, and it's the same routine every day. So... I don't really have a chance to find out if learning new things is hard, because I'm not really doing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel mentally foggy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. That's actually why I came in, to be honest. My head just doesn't stay on task anymore. Like, I'll be doing inventory, or trying to study for this supervisor certification, and I just keep reading the same line over and over. Or I'll walk all the way down to the supply closet at work and completely forget what I even went in there for. So yeah, foggy is a good word for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or motivation to do things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. My energy is about the same as it usually is. I still do the stuff I normally like to do when I get home from work. I haven't really lost motivation for my regular hobbies or anything like that. It's really just the focus and the brain fog that's been the issue.\n\nDoctor: Are you having difficulty performing tasks that require mental effort, such as planning or problem-solving?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's pretty much exactly what I was just saying. Whenever I have to actually use my brain for something like doing the inventory or studying for that certification, my head just doesn't stay on task. It's like I mentioned—I just end up reading the same sentence over and over, or I completely lose my train of thought when I'm trying to figure out what I need from the supply closet. So, yeah, trying to focus on anything that takes real mental effort is definitely where I'm having the most trouble right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself struggling to find the right words when you're trying to express yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I have a hard time finding words or anything like that. When I'm talking to people, I can usually explain what I mean just fine. It's really just the focus and the memory stuff that's bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to make decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really just ties back to what I was saying a minute ago. Making a decision is tough mostly because my head just doesn't stay on task. Like I mentioned with the inventory or trying to study for that supervisor certification... I just can't hold onto a thought long enough to figure things out. It's exactly like forgetting what I went to the supply closet for. When your brain keeps blanking out like that, it definitely makes deciding what to do a lot harder.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like I was just saying, I'm not disoriented or anything. I'm not confused about what's going on... it's just the focusing and the memory stuff that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's not like a noise or people walking down the hall pull my attention away. The problem is more... internal, I guess. My brain just kind of loses track of what I'm doing on its own, rather than me actually getting distracted by my surroundings.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble learning new things?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been trying to learn anything new lately. I just do my job cleaning the building, and it's the same routine every day. So... I don't really have a chance to find out if learning new things is hard, because I'm not really doing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel mentally foggy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. That's actually why I came in, to be honest. My head just doesn't stay on task anymore. Like, I'll be doing inventory, or trying to study for this supervisor certification, and I just keep reading the same line over and over. Or I'll walk all the way down to the supply closet at work and completely forget what I even went in there for. So yeah, foggy is a good word for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or motivation to do things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. My energy is about the same as it usually is. I still do the stuff I normally like to do when I get home from work. I haven't really lost motivation for my regular hobbies or anything like that. It's really just the focus and the brain fog that's been the issue.\n\nDoctor: Are you having difficulty performing tasks that require mental effort, such as planning or problem-solving?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's pretty much exactly what I was just saying. Whenever I have to actually use my brain for something like doing the inventory or studying for that certification, my head just doesn't stay on task. It's like I mentioned—I just end up reading the same sentence over and over, or I completely lose my train of thought when I'm trying to figure out what I need from the supply closet. So, yeah, trying to focus on anything that takes real mental effort is definitely where I'm having the most trouble right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself struggling to find the right words when you're trying to express yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I have a hard time finding words or anything like that. When I'm talking to people, I can usually explain what I mean just fine. It's really just the focus and the memory stuff that's bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to make decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really just ties back to what I was saying a minute ago. Making a decision is tough mostly because my head just doesn't stay on task. Like I mentioned with the inventory or trying to study for that supervisor certification... I haven't exactly been keeping track of it like that. It just happens enough that it's messing with my work and my studying. But, yeah, I couldn't tell you for certain if there are days where my head is totally clear or not.\n\nDoctor: Are these problems with focus and memory something that happen consistently, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm just not really sure. Like I was saying, I don't really track it, so... I don't know if it comes and goes in waves or anything like that. It just feels like whenever I actually need my brain to work, like for studying or doing the supply inventory, it's a struggle. So I haven't really noticed a specific pattern to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these problems with your focus and memory are always present, or do they come and go at different times?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I feel like I'm repeating myself a bit here. Like I just said, I really don't know if it's always there or if it comes and goes. I just haven't paid enough attention to the timing of it to give you a clear answer. I only really notice it when I'm actually trying to get something specific done.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these problems with your focus and memory are always present when you're trying to concentrate?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I guess that's the best way to put it. It's just getting really frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks at work or in your daily routine because of these memory issues?\n\nPatient: I mean, I still get the actual cleaning done. But I'm definitely slower than I used to be. I end up second-guessing my maintenance logs so much that... well, I've pretty much stopped pushing for that supervisor track I mentioned. And outside of work, yeah, it's affecting things too. I've been skipping the neighborhood clean-ups I used to volunteer for, and I just don't go to family get-togethers anymore, even though I always used to make time for them.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble focusing or paying attention to things you're doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's... well, like I mentioned a minute ago, that's exactly what's been happening. My head just won't stay on task. If I'm doing inventory, or trying to read through those certification materials, my brain just doesn't lock in and I end up reading the same line over and over. Or, you know, the supply closet thing I said earlier—just walking in and completely forgetting what I was supposed to be paying attention to. So, yeah, focusing is definitely a big issue for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling disoriented or unsure of what's going on around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I always know where I am and what's happening around me. It's not like I feel lost... it's really just that my brain won't hold onto a thought. But I'm never actually confused about my surroundings or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel confused about things that are happening around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was just saying, I'm not disoriented or anything. I'm not confused about what's going on... it's just the focusing and the memory stuff that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's not like a noise or people walking down the hall pull my attention away. The problem is more... internal, I guess. My brain just kind of loses track of what I'm doing on its own, rather than me actually getting distracted by my surroundings.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble learning new things?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been trying to learn anything new lately. I just do my job cleaning the building, and it's the same routine every day. So... I don't really have a chance to find out if learning new things is hard, because I'm not really doing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel mentally foggy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. That's actually why I came in, to be honest. My head just doesn't stay on task anymore. Like, I'll be doing inventory, or trying to study for this supervisor certification, and I just keep reading the same line over and over. Or I'll walk all the way down to the supply closet at work and completely forget what I even went in there for. So yeah, foggy is a good word for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or motivation to do things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. My energy is about the same as it usually is. I still do the stuff I normally like to do when I get home from work. I haven't really lost motivation for my regular hobbies or anything like that. It's really just the focus and the brain fog that's been the issue.\n\nDoctor: Are you having difficulty performing tasks that require mental effort, such as planning or problem-solving?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's pretty much exactly what I was just saying. Whenever I have to actually use my brain for something like doing the inventory or studying for that certification, my head just doesn't stay on task. It's like I mentioned—I just end up reading the same sentence over and over, or I completely lose my train of thought when I'm trying to figure out what I need from the supply closet. So, yeah, trying to focus on anything that takes real mental effort is definitely where I'm having the most trouble right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself struggling to find the right words when you're trying to express yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I have a hard time finding words or anything like that. When I'm talking to people, I can usually explain what I mean just fine. It's really just the focus and the memory stuff that's bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to make decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really just ties back to what I was saying a minute ago. Making a decision is tough mostly because my head just doesn't stay on task. Like I mentioned with the inventory or trying to study for that supervisor certification... I just can't hold onto a thought long enough to figure things out. It's exactly like forgetting what I went to the supply closet for. When your brain keeps blanking out like that, it definitely makes deciding what to do a lot harder.\n\nDoctor: Are these problems with focus and memory something you experience every day?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's every single day. I haven't exactly been keeping track of it like that. It just happens enough that it's messing with my work and my studying. But, yeah, I couldn't tell you for certain if there are days where my head is totally clear or not.\n\nDoctor: Are these problems with focus and memory something that happen consistently, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm just not really sure. Like I was saying, I don't really track it, so... I don't know if it comes and goes in waves or anything like that. It just feels like whenever I actually need my brain to work, like for studying or doing the supply inventory, it's a struggle. So I haven't really noticed a specific pattern to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these problems with your focus and memory are always present, or do they come and go at different times?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I feel like I'm repeating myself a bit here. Like I just said, I really don't know if it's always there or if it comes and goes. I just haven't paid enough attention to the timing of it to give you a clear answer. I only really notice it when I'm actually trying to get something specific done.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these problems with your focus and memory are always present when you're trying to concentrate?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I guess that's the best way to put it. I still don't really have a yes or no for you. I just know it happens enough to be a real problem. I don't really have a better way to explain it than that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these problems with your focus and memory?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's been going on for about three years now. It started right around the time the contractor changed our schedule at work. It hasn't really changed, it's just... always kind of been there since it started.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Or I'll walk all the way down to the supply closet at work and completely forget what I even went in there for. So yeah, foggy is a good word for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or motivation to do things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. My energy is about the same as it usually is. I still do the stuff I normally like to do when I get home from work. I haven't really lost motivation for my regular hobbies or anything like that. It's really just the focus and the brain fog that's been the issue.\n\nDoctor: Are you having difficulty performing tasks that require mental effort, such as planning or problem-solving?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's pretty much exactly what I was just saying. Whenever I have to actually use my brain for something like doing the inventory or studying for that certification, my head just doesn't stay on task. It's like I mentioned—I just end up reading the same sentence over and over, or I completely lose my train of thought when I'm trying to figure out what I need from the supply closet. So, yeah, trying to focus on anything that takes real mental effort is definitely where I'm having the most trouble right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself struggling to find the right words when you're trying to express yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I have a hard time finding words or anything like that. When I'm talking to people, I can usually explain what I mean just fine. It's really just the focus and the memory stuff that's bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to make decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really just ties back to what I was saying a minute ago. Making a decision is tough mostly because my head just doesn't stay on task. Like I mentioned with the inventory or trying to study for that supervisor certification... I just can't hold onto a thought long enough to figure things out. It's exactly like forgetting what I went to the supply closet for. When your brain keeps blanking out like that, it definitely makes deciding what to do a lot harder.\n\nDoctor: Are these problems with focus and memory something you experience every day?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's every single day. I haven't exactly been keeping track of it like that. It just happens enough that it's messing with my work and my studying. But, yeah, I couldn't tell you for certain if there are days where my head is totally clear or not.\n\nDoctor: Are these problems with focus and memory something that happen consistently, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: I guess I'm just not really sure. Like I was saying, I don't really track it, so... I don't know if it comes and goes in waves or anything like that. It just feels like whenever I actually need my brain to work, like for studying or doing the supply inventory, it's a struggle.
